### Log review 2026-10-16 00:29: CSA_00B4 helper $013FB2 on the interpreter, scan of all unregistered call targets
 * User confirmed that the Zak / Lara cutscene (CSA_00B2, table `$013ECA`) now plays without log lines ('hat funktioniert'). New `~/interp.txt`: only `$013FB2` (CSA_00B4+0x12), journal: the same two `RTS to` lines as before right after it (`$002304` ScrollCameraPlus+0xC2 expected GameLoop+0x42, `$0021A4` CheckForLadderClimb_loc1 expected RunGameLoop+0xC): they come WITH an interpreter run of an unregistered subroutine entry (the interpreter's return handling leaves a stale address), not from the quick load.
 * Cause: `CSA_00B4` does `bsr.b $13FB2` and then falls through into the same code (`trap #0` + data word, `move.w #4,$26(a5)`, then `$13FB2: movem.l a5,-(a7) ... jmp Sleep`): a subroutine entry in the middle of a function, the generator does not split there (same class as `$00673E` / `0x0024 (colour)58`). `$13FB2` is an instruction boundary (verified: bsr.b $0A from $13FA6 lands there, the preceding instruction ends exactly at $13FB2).
 * Proactive scan (all `func_table_jsr/jmp/call(0xADDR, ...)` constants in `src/recomp/*.c` that are not registered): 8 targets. Seeded as valid instruction boundaries: `$013FB2`, `$0145E2` (CSA_00F2, same helper pattern), `$00E782` (copy loop, bsr.w from NoleWarp `$00E63C`), `$019FB0` (CustomRoomActions1: four `move.b #$7F`, bra.w EndCustomRoomAction, bsr.b from `$019FCC`). NOT seeded: `$00B276` and `$017127` (callers sit inside data tables decoded as code: `$00B23C` is in `BlockToTiles` data, `$017127` is odd) and `$FF1850` / `$FF1858` (RAM jump stubs handled by `patch_ramstub`). Rebuilt: the four functions are registered natively (named `CSA_00B4_ShowSpriteStep`, `CSA_00F2_ShowSpriteStep`, `NoleWarp_CopyWords`, `CustomRoomActions1_SetCells7F`), Massan replay equal to the reference (25 / 25), no `interp:` / `RTS to` lines. Not reproducible headless (needs the story state of that cutscene): user check open.
 * Method for the future (cheap): after each build run the scan of unregistered constant call targets (snippet in this section: regex `func_table_(jsr|jmp|call)\((0x[0-9A-Fa-f]{6}),` over `src/recomp/recomp_*_*.c` against the `func_table_register(0x...` list in `recomp_funcs.h`; named functions appear as `Name_XXXXXX` there, so compare against the `= 0xXXXXXX` enum lines too).

### Widescreen / full height off for a moment: door entry, Start menu fades, Gaia statue (user recording 2026-10-16 00:39)
 * User report (recording `rec_20261016_0039`, WIDE 16 + TALL 1): margins and full height briefly missing when entering buildings in Mercator, when opening / leaving the Start menu and while the Statue of Gaia is used. Replay: `docs/replays/mercator_doors_menu_gaia.*` (exact with `LANDSTALKER_QUICK=1 LANDSTALKER_QUICK_SLOTS=0,19`). `LANDSTALKER_WIDE_DEBUG=1` shows the map rule: (1) three entries through floor doors: 3 frames 'not map' each (743-746, 1742-1745, 2247-2250): `HandleFloorDoorNW $0061CA` sleeps (door animation) for more than 4 frames without UpdateSprites before `WarpToRoom` comes onto the stack; (2) menu open: `FadeOutToDarkness $008EF4` inside `CheckForMenuOpen $007624` = the map fades out but counted as not map from the first frame (2828); menu close: `FadeInFromDarkness $008EB4` inside `$007766` (8 frames, map fading in) + 2 frames until the first UpdateSprites; (3) `GaiaEffect $016712` (called from `HandleAttack_SetHitReaction+0x8C`) runs about 120 frames without UpdateSprites: margins black for the whole effect, twice.
 * Fix (`tools/patch_wide.py`, `lk_map_decide`): on the call stack now also count as map: `HandleFloorDoorNE / SE / SW / NW` ($00610A / $00613E / $006184 / $0061CA) and `GaiaEffect` ($016712) (with the usual 30-frame grace period); `FadeOutToDarkness` directly inside `$007624` = map WITHOUT grace (the menu follows); `FadeInFromDarkness` directly inside `$007766` = map WITH grace (the first UpdateSprites pass comes 2 frames after the fade returns). Full height uses the same rule (`lk_map_decide`). Result on the replay: the only 'not map' stretches left are the real menu (2845-2994, 3190-3349) and the logos / title at the start; screenshots of the fade-out, fade-in and the Gaia effect show margins and full height; game RAM unchanged (rule is renderer-only, 41 / 41 checkpoints equal). Other effects with their own loops (e.g. DoVisualEffect $00E110: warp pads, tree warp, red flash) are NOT added yet: report them if they show the same flicker.
 * Why 'the second entry did not flicker' (user): the three entries have the same 3-frame gap in the replay; probably only visible when the door animation timing differs; the fix removes all three.

### Windows package (2026-10-16): cross-build, Wine tests, what is verified and what is not
 * Design (variant A like the AppImage): `packaging/make_windows_zip.py` builds `Project-Friday-windows-x64.zip` (132 MB) on Linux (also in CI): `Project-Friday.exe` (launcher, `packaging/launcher_win.c`, console program: starts `python\python.exe firstrun.py` with PYTHONHOME / PYTHONUTF8 set), bundled Python 3.13.5 embeddable (without `python313._pth`, so the tools find their neighbours; capstone 5.0.6 win_amd64 wheel unpacked into `Lib/site-packages`), zig 0.16.0 for Windows, SDL2 2.32.10 MinGW development files + `SDL2.dll`, pristine genrecomp / GPGX, patch tools, licences. `firstrun.py` is OS-aware (`WIN`): data in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Project-Friday`, ROM dialog / message boxes through PowerShell + Windows Forms, progress as text in the console, `zig cc -target x86_64-windows-gnu`, MinGW SDL2 flags (`-Dmain=SDL_main`, `libSDL2main.a`, `libSDL2.dll.a`), `SDL2.dll` copied next to the game, `subprocess` instead of `execv`, `HOME` = user profile for the `~/.landstalker_*` settings.
 * Portability fixes in OUR patches (the only two things that did not compile for Windows): `_setjmp` / `_longjmp` (patch_unwind / patch_calltrace: `LK_SETJMP` / `LK_LONGJMP` macros), `mkdir(dir, 0755)` (patch_shot: `_mkdir`). 163 of 163 files compile with `zig cc -target x86_64-windows-gnu`, `zigbuild.py` names the output `landstalker.exe`.
 * VERIFIED: the cross-built `landstalker.exe` runs headless under Wine (Steam's Proton 10.0 `files/bin/wine64`, throw-away `WINEPREFIX`, `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy`) and the RAM dumps of the Massan replay (1500 frames) are byte-identical to the Linux build (25 of 25). The bundled python.exe + capstone run under Wine. The whole first run (`Project-Friday.exe --no-gui --rom ... --build-only`) under Wine goes through all 54 patch / generate steps and compiles 160 of 163 files.
 * NOT verified: a real Windows PC. Under Wine 3 of 163 files fail with `error: CacheCheckFailed` (always the first ones of the compile pool, the set varies): zig's cache code calls CreateDirectory on existing directories and this Wine returns STATUS_FILE_IS_A_DIRECTORY (`c00000ba`, seen with `WINEDEBUG=+file` on `C:\windows` / `C:\windows\inf`) where Windows returns a name collision, zig reports `Unexpected` ('unable to open global cache directory'). It also reproduces with fresh cache dirs and a C: location, serially, so it is a Wine incompatibility, not a package bug; on real Windows zig is in wide use. Safeguards added anyway (`tools/zigbuild.py`): one serial warm-up compile, 4 attempts per file (the last with a private cache), a serial second pass for failed files. Not tested at all: the PowerShell file dialog / message boxes (no PowerShell under Wine), SmartScreen / antivirus reactions to the unsigned exe, the console look. The release text calls the package experimental.
 * Test recipe (Deck): Proton's wine64 is already installed with Steam (`~/.steam/steam/steamapps/common/Proton 10.0/files/bin/wine64`); `export WINEPREFIX=<scratch> WINEDEBUG=-all LD_LIBRARY_PATH="$P/lib64:$P/lib:$LD_LIBRARY_PATH"`; pass Unix paths directly (`/tmp/...`) or `Z:` paths; create `--ram-dump` directories first; nothing under Steam is modified.

### Windows package under Proton on the Deck (user report 2026-10-16)
 * User ran `Project-Friday.exe` through Proton: no file dialog, only a cmd-like console asking for the ROM path, exit code 1. Cause: on Windows `Ui` used PowerShell for the dialogs; Wine / Proton has no PowerShell, so `gui` was off, the console `input()` hit EOF (no stdin) and the program ended with code 1. Fix (`firstrun.py`): the native Windows 'Open file' dialog and the message boxes through `ctypes` (`comdlg32.GetOpenFileNameW`, `user32.MessageBoxW`; the bundled embeddable Python has ctypes, checked under Wine), PowerShell only as a second choice, and a clear message when no ROM was chosen. Under Wine the dialog opens (the process waits). The first start shows the notice as a message box before the dialog. The fixed package is in `landstalker/dist/Project-Friday-windows-x64.zip` on the Deck (built locally, v0.4.0 on GitHub still has the PowerShell dialog, which is fine on real Windows).
 * zig under Wine: the compile step is NOT reliable there. In a fresh `WINEPREFIX` EVERY `zig.exe cc -c a.c` (even a 3-line file, any flags, cwd, cache dir, spawned from bash or from python) ends with `error: CacheCheckFailed`; in the earlier full runs 160 of 163 files compiled and 3 failed (no stable pattern found, trace shows `NtCreateFile "tmp\<id>"` -> c000003a and Wine's c00000ba for CreateFile on existing directories). Do not debug this further: it is a Wine problem; the AppImage is the Deck path, the Windows zip needs a real Windows PC for the compile step. Everything before the compile (dialogs, ROM check, 54 patch / generate steps) and the cross-built game itself work under Wine.

### Windows package checked on a REAL Windows runner (2026-10-16, GitHub Actions `windows-latest`)
 * New workflow `.github/workflows/windows-check.yml` (manual: `workflow_dispatch`, input `tag` = a release tag): downloads `Project-Friday-windows-x64.zip` of that release, lays this commit's `firstrun.py` / `tools/` / `build.sh` / `recomp.json` over it (bundle = zig, Python, SDL2, genrecomp of the release), then runs `Project-Friday.exe --selftest` and `--selftest-build`. `--selftest` = compile + link + run a tiny SDL2 program with the bundled zig and the MinGW SDL2 files; `--selftest-build` (`firstrun.py`, `zigbuild.py --no-game`) = the 47 patch steps that need neither the ROM nor generated code + compile of genrecomp and Genesis Plus GX (61 files), in a throw-away folder; the same check runs on Linux in 16 s.
 * RESULT on the real Windows runner (run 38852114274, tag `v0.4.1-rc1`): the launcher exe starts the bundled Python 3.13.5 (ctypes `windll` present, capstone 5.0.6), `--selftest` ok (2 min 37 s, cold zig cache: zig builds its runtime pieces once), `--selftest-build` ok (34 s for patches + 61 compiles). So `CacheCheckFailed` is a Wine / Proton problem only: zig's cache works on real Windows. First attempt failed only because my selftest program used `int main(void)` while the MinGW SDL header renames `main` to `SDL_main` (conflicting types): now `int main(int argc, char *argv[])`.
 * Still NOT verified on Windows: the file dialog (`comdlg32`) and message boxes on a real screen, the compile of the generated game code and the link of `landstalker.exe` (needs the ROM), running the game (window, sound, pad), SmartScreen / antivirus reactions. Under Proton on the Deck the compile step fails (user confirmed: `CacheCheckFailed`).
 * Release: `v0.4.1-rc1` is a PRE-release (tag with a dash) containing the ctypes dialog fix and the new self-tests; v0.4.0 stays the latest normal release.

Final: Light 90 / Medium 120 / Strong 150, weight = gaussian x colour similarity, 3x3-flat fast path (6 ms -> ~2 ms), UI guard as before (menu frame: 0 pixels changed). Tool: `displaytest --dedither IN.ppm OUT.ppm` (`DD_BIL=range,gate`, `DD_STRONG=1` = Medium, `DD_BLEND=1` = Strong); PNG -> PPM with `ffmpeg`. The headless screenshot (`LANDSTALKER_SHOT`) does NOT apply the filter. User confirmed 'viel besser'.

## Nigel on an object on a moving platform (user recording `vase_platform_room444`, 2026-10-16)
User: Nigel picks up a vase (X) in room 444, puts it on a moving platform; standing on the vase he is NOT carried although he is carried on the platform itself. Checked with the replay (`docs/replays/vase_platform_room444`, quick load at frame 647: `LANDSTALKER_QSREQ="l647"`, replays exactly) and per-frame dumps (`--ram-dump DIR:1`, entity fields: +9 move direction, +2C action, +2D step flags, +30 supporter, Z +12):
 * Room 444: slot 1 and 2 (type $15) are platforms moving along X / Y (+9 = 1, +2D bit 3 / 2 / 1 / 2 steps), the vase is slot 3 (type $11). Put down on slot 1 its +30 becomes `$0080` (= slot 1), and it follows the platform exactly (x differs by 1 px at most).
 * Nigel on the vase: `$FF5430` = `$0180` (the vase), but the vase has +9 = 0 and +2D = 0 for the whole ride: it is moved by UpdateAllLinkedEntities `$0190D4` -> UpdateLinkedEntity `$019114` (copies the platform's flags for ONE step through MoveEntityWithSupport `$01914C` and then restores its own +9) and has no move flags afterwards. HandlePlayerLinkedEntity `$0017EE` only looks at the entity directly under Nigel, finds nothing, so Nigel stays; after a few frames the supporter check drops the link and he falls (frame 2204, z `$70` -> `$40`).
 * Fix (optional, `tools/patch_carry.py`, Options > Gameplay > Ride Objects, default Off): wrapper of `$0017EE`: if `$FF5430` points at a slot whose own +30 is a slot offset (`$80..$780`) and whose +9 low nibble is 0, `$FF5430` is set to that supporter for the call and restored. Clean test (input cut at frame 1871, Nigel stands still on the vase): Off = Nigel x stays `$01B6`, falls at ~1894; On = x follows the vase (`$01BB`, `$01C1`, ... 300+ frames, Z stays `$70`). Replay with the fix Off is unchanged (checkpoints equal), with On it diverges from ~1880 because the user walked along by hand.
 * Not covered: Z sync (`SyncPlayerHeightWithLinkedEntity $001858` reads +2D bits 4 / 5 of the entity under Nigel: a vase on a lifting platform would need the same trick), objects carried by objects (more than one step), enemies. Not seen in the recording.

## Full height: garbage band after a long text box (ship after the island map; user recording `ship_band_garbage`, 2026-10-16)
 * User: on the ship (room 543, after the island map with the red dots) garbage at the bottom of the picture. Replay (exact) screenshots: frames ~3590-3612 show a stripe of mixed tiles in the band below the ship, right after the text box ('We still have a ways to go') closes; it vanishes at 3615 when the first scroll step runs `LoadBottomTiles $00A698` by itself.
 * Cause: UpdateSprites `$0178C8` did not run for ~146 frames (the cutscene text box was open, first call at 3587), the game changed plane A below its own picture meanwhile and nothing reloaded the extra full-height strips until the camera moved.
 * Fix (`patch_wide.py lk_strip_extend`, only with full height on): if UpdateSprites was not called for more than 12 frames, the first map pass reloads all four strip loaders (`lk_wide_refill = 1`, the same private-queue mechanism as at fade-in; no game time). Replay exact with the fix, frames 3590-3614 clean. Headless-verified; user check open (ship, dialogues in other places).
 * **Brick stub in room 632 (frames ~806-826) FIXED the same day:** it was NOT real map data. The block map is 74 x 74 words (`$FF7C02`, stride 148, background layer `+$2AC8`); our extra full-height strips (4 steps down) walk past the end of the array, and `BlockToTiles $00A748` then read the BACKGROUND layer (`$FFA712...`) as foreground (blocks with row > 73; seen: 10 distinct addresses around frames 780-803). Fix (`patch_wide.py lk_strip_extend`): while one of our strips runs, a block address outside `$FF7C02..$FFA6C9` is replaced by the empty cell 0,0 (a6 difference added back at the next call, because the loader steps a6 relative to it). Replay exact, stub gone (frames 806-824); `LANDSTALKER_TALL_DEBUG=1` prints `tall: block outside the map`. Column wrap (x > 73 reads the next row) is not detected. User check open.

## Ship: margins / full height lost after the island map (user recording `ship_band_garbage`, 2026-10-16)
 * User: coming back from the island map to the ship, widescreen and full height were off for a moment (frames ~3375-3420), then garbage in the band.
 * `LANDSTALKER_WIDE_DEBUG=1`: frame 3402 'not map', stack `UpdateSprites -> EB_TriggerCutscene -> CSA_00D3 -> WarpToRoom+0x4 ($006212) -> FadeInFromDarkness -> Sleep`. Cause: `lk_picture` (island map shown, set at `DisplayIslandMap $03E654`) is cleared only at `$002996` / `$00620E` / `$0178C8` ENTRY; CSA_00D3 jumps into the middle of WarpToRoom (`$006212`) while UpdateSprites is already on the stack, so the flag stayed set for ~45 frames (rule: picture = never a map), and the fade-in band reload (`$008EB4`, skipped while `lk_picture`) did not run either (the band garbage at 3423).
 * Fix (`patch_wide.py`): `$006212` also clears `lk_picture`. Replay exact; frames 3375-3430: fade-in complete with margins and band, reload log `tall: reload at fade-in $008EB4 frame 3401`. User check open.
 * Room 197 case (WIDESCREEN_CASES #1, arch floating in the void at the lower left): checked again 2026-10-16 with WIDE 16 + TALL, 48 + TALL: STILL visible (real map data of a detached island, not an array overrun); not touched by the out-of-array fix. Idea: blank blocks that are not connected to the camera block over the block map (flood fill) in the extra strips.

## Item descriptions in the Start menu (optional 'Item Info', user wish 2026-10-16: Saturn Stone had no explanation)
 * `tools/patch_iteminfo.py`, Options > Gameplay > Item Info Off / On (default Off, `~/.landstalker_iteminfo`, env `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO`, `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO_DEBUG=1` prints ids / stacks). Cursor hooks: wrapper of InventoryMenu_UpdateCursorAt `$00D714` (Use list: item = `$FF2C5C + ((scroll $FF2C06 + row $FF2C0C) * 2 + column $FF2C0A)`) and `$00EDAA` (Equip grid: column `$FF2C18`, row `$FF2C1A`, item = ROM `EquipInventoryLayout $00ECE0 + row * 5 + col`, bit 7 = nothing; only owned items: GetItemQtyAndMaxQty `$022ED0` d1 >= 0). Drawing: `$024998` + `$0234A8` rebuild the bar box, the three words are printed again and the description one text line lower (`$FF1129` + $10, game text engine); `lk_ii_leave` (patch_options.py, bar loop) restores the plain bar. The Use list does NOT run under CheckForMenuOpen `$007624` on the stack but `GameLoop -> $00774A -> $00D3BE ...`, the Equip grid `$007624 -> $00EAF2`; `lk_ii_menu()` accepts both.
 * The font has no '%': armour reads 'Enemy damage x0.9'. Texts come from the game code (this session) and from https://shrines.rpgclassics.com/genesis/landstalker/equipment.shtml (user's tip): ring facts verified in code: Saturn `$0F` / Venus `$10` = sword charge step `$40` / `$60` instead of `$20` (IncrementSwordCharge `$017858`: 2x / 3x), Mars `$0D` = AddStatusEffect `$017776` effects 0 (poison) and 2 (paralysis; the web page says poison only), Moon `$0E` = effect 1 (the reversed-controls status that Mind Repair cures; the web page calls it confusion); boots: Fireproof = lava, Iron = spikes, Spike = ice, Healing = heals while walking; armour table `$00770E` (Steel x0.9 .. Hyper x0.5; the page says defence 2x..5x). Swords: charge attacks from the page. Items without a text show the plain bar.
 * Headless-verified (Use list: EkeEke 'Restores health', Detox Grass 'Cures poison', Golden Statue none; Equip grid: Magic Sword, Steel Breast, Saturn Stone, empty cells none); the Gameplay page now has 8 rows and scrolls (arrows generalised to pages >= 3). User check open. More texts: add rows to `lk_ii_tab` (max ~34 characters).
 * **Update (user recording `rec_20261016_2245`):** (1) flicker when switching Use <-> Equip in the bar with an item under the cursor: MenuBarRight `$024916` / MenuBarLeft `$024952` redraw the list and call the cursor update, the hook drew the description, the next bar pass (`lk_ii_leave`) removed it again, every frame; now no description while one of those two is on the call stack (`lk_ii_tabdraw`); `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO_DEBUG=1` shows one draw per cursor change and none per tab switch. (2) Divider: a 1 pixel line (palette nibble `$B` = white, `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO_LINE=n` tries others) between the words and the description, written straight into the text box tile buffer (`$FF3900`, 4 bpp, 16 pixel text line = 8 pixel columns of 64 bytes, line stride `$900`, pixel row 14) before the DMA; the description starts at x `$10`. (3) More texts from https://shrines.rpgclassics.com/genesis/landstalker/items.shtml (user's tip): EkeEke, Dahl, Restoration, Gaia / Golden Statue, Key, Idol Stone, jewels, Lithograph, Safety Pass, Bell, Pawn Ticket, Armlet, Buyer Card, Oracle Stone, Short Cake, Death Statue, Garlic, Whistle, Sun Stone, Axe Magic, Casino Ticket, Gola's Eye / Nail / Horn / Fang, Lantern, Logs (Blue Ribbon, Spell Book: 'purpose unknown', no text; Blue / Yellow / Green Jewel assumed the same as Red / Purple). The recording does not replay exactly with the current build (item info was On while recording): not added to docs/replays.
 * **Update 2 (user: 'beim Wechseln auf ein Item mit Beschreibung haengt es kurz'):** the game's text engine costs ~0.14 ms per character here (`lk_menu_print` of the whole bar + description ~6 ms on a PC, measured with `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO_DEBUG=1`), and DMACopyTextboxTiles `$02355A` ends in FlushDMACopyQueue = a wait for the next VBlank (one extra frame inside the cursor step). Fix (`patch_iteminfo.py` v2): the draw is a SLICED JOB (12 characters per cursor update, longest slice ~1.7-3.5 ms), nothing is DMA'd before it is complete, the plain bar (words only) is cached and every description starts from that copy (only the description is rendered), finished buffers are cached per item (a revisit = plain copy of the buffer + queued DMA, no rendering, no wait); the DMA is only QUEUED (`QueueDMAOp $0002C6` + `EnableDMAQueueProcessing $0002A2`). Pitfall found on the way: QueueDMAOp counts WORDS: the text box DMA with the inventory open is `$0D80` words = 6912 bytes (three 16 pixel lines of `$900`); caching only `$0D80` BYTES left the tail of the previous description on the screen. The comma is CHR `$41` (guessed, works). A description appears ~5-12 frames after the cursor lands (first visit), instantly on a revisit.

## Map Behind Text (2026-10-17)
 * Recording `rec_20261017_1249` (WIDE=16, TALL=1, Massan dialogue at frame ~800): the text box sits in a black band, only the box itself is purple. Cause: dialogue = window plane over the whole line from line 160; pixels outside the box are black window tiles (non-transparent index) and the widescreen margins are blanked by `lk_wide_margins`. First try 'replace transparent pixels only' fixed just the margins; the black cells inside the 320 px are real black pixels, so the rule is: pass 1 (window) pixels stay unless transparent or part of the black run from the left / right end of the line (`lk_tbox_line` in `vdp_render.c`, pass 2 = `reg[18] = 0` and `clip[0]` full width, `render_bg` + `render_obj`).
 * Verified: RAM checkpoint file of the replay byte-identical with `LANDSTALKER_TBOX=0` and `=1`. Test aid `LANDSTALKER_TBOX_DEBUG=1`.

## Gaia effect without freezing the game: 'Live Gaia Effect' (2026-10-19, dev, Sonnet 5.5)
 * User wish: the Statue of Gaia / Gaia Sword effect freezes the whole game for ~2 s (130 frames); keep the effect, keep playing. Worry: leaving the effect area in big rooms / enemies out of view.
 * Findings (disassembly `combat.asm`, replay `docs/replays/mercator_doors_menu_gaia.*`): `HandleAttack $015C1C` damages EVERY enemy of the room first (loop at `$0160B8`, no screen test), only then calls `GaiaEffect $016712`, which sets the Gaia sword graphics (VRAM `$F180` = tile `$78C`) + 2 palette words (`g_Pal2Base+$1A/$1C`), saves VSRAM, rumbles and runs `GaiaEffectLoop $016794` (never returns until all rocks are gone; nothing else of `GameLoop` runs). The rocks are 41 screen-space sprites (tile `$C78C / $C79C / $C7AC`, 4 x 4 tiles) in the RAM sprite table from the first free slot >= 16, chained slot 15 -> rocks -> slot 16; they do not belong to the world, so 'running out of the effect' does not exist and out-of-view enemies are hit anyway.
 * Built (`tools/patch_gaia.py`, option `Options > Quality of Life > Live Gaia Effect`, `~/.landstalker_gaia`, env `LANDSTALKER_GAIA=0|1`, default Off): `GaiaEffect` is replaced (graphics + palette + rumble + rock init, then `func_table_rts()`), the rocks are moved by a C port of `$0167D6 / $016848 / $01685E / $0168F4` once per game pass in a wrapper of `LoadSprites $00401C` (rocks written into the first free slots after the entity sprites, slot 15 link = first rock, last rock link = $10; own RNG, the game's seed is untouched) ; no VSRAM shake in live mode (user: it stutters while walking, 2026-10-19). The effect ends like the original (2 cycles, all rocks cleared), on a room change, when START is pressed (`CheckForMenuOpen` wrapper: rocks hidden, graphics / palette / VSRAM restored before the menu opens) and before a Quick Load. The own renderer gets the Gaia sword palette through `lk_gaia_live()`.
 * Verified headless (replay with the statue at frame 4203, WIDE 16 + TALL): option Off = checkpoint file IDENTICAL to a build without the patch; On: Nigel walks while the rocks fall (screenshots 4214 .. 4400), rocks have the Gaia colours (136,102,34) not the Magic ones, the effect ends (0 rock pixels at 4400), the second statue use (frame ~4500) works, no `RTS to` / `no function` lines; Options page shows 'Live Gaia Effect' as 7th row and toggling writes the file. Pitfalls met: (1) own renderer ON used the real sword byte -> orange (Magic) rocks (LESSONS 65); (2) replacement function without `func_table_rts()` (LESSONS 64); (3) opening the Start menu while the effect runs left garbage tiles / a stuck scroll arrow after closing; cause not found (all effect parts switched off did not change it), the rule 'end the effect cleanly when START is pressed' gives the same state the original is in after the effect. The headless menu tests (injected START / B) end on a black screen even without a statue, so they prove nothing either way: USER CHECK.
 * Not handled / ideas: dialogue boxes while rocks fall (rocks stay frozen), Quick Save during the effect (the effect state is not saved), widescreen margins (rocks cover the 320 px window only, like the original), a second statue while one runs restarts the rocks.

Remaining differences in `rec_20261019_2044` are the rock frames of the Gaia effect (1814-1915, 3658-3759, 3780-3878: the VDP's 20-sprites-per-line limit, known) and 3260-3285 / 3360-3389 (not looked at).

## Own renderer: the state as the VDP receives it, VBlank snapshot (2026-10-20 night, dev c0b0052, Sonnet 5.5)
- **Idea:** the game hands everything to the VDP inside `HandleVBlankInterrupt $0011DE` (`bclr #7 g_InterruptFlags` -> `RefreshVDPSpriteTable` = sprite table DMA from `$FF0550`, `ProcessQueuedVDPActions` = VRAM copy queue / DMA ops incl. hscroll, then `EnableDisplay`, window position register, `sub_13B6` sound). A func_table wrapper (`patch_ownvb.py`) copies the work RAM right after the handler returns; the interrupt is delivered by `recomp_m68k_interrupt` -> `func_table_call(vector)` so the wrapper sees it.
- **Check 1 (scroll):** `LANDSTALKER_OWN_VBLOG`: `g_HScrollA/B $FF0100/$FF0102` and `g_VScrollA/B $FF0500/$FF0502` of the snapshot equal the VDP's hscroll table / VSRAM in 2599 of 2600 frames (`rec_20261019_2215`; the one miss is the frame after the quick load). Using them in the picture was WORSE only in the Gaia statue frames (Mercator replay, room 456, frames 4202-4203 / 4362-4365 / 4507-4508: 27000 - 34000 differing pixels): the original `GaiaEffectLoop` shakes VSRAM directly.
- **Check 2 (hint line):** first version used the snapshot for the text box hint line: frames 595 / 1335 of the Ryuma replay (box opening) had a black lower half and no box (16000 pixels): `SetVDPForTextbox $02347E` writes `g_HIntLineReg $FF0FC4` and the VDP register right after `WaitUntilVBlank`, i.e. in the main code after the handler. Fixed: live RAM for the hint.
- **Check 3 (sprite table):** VRAM sprite table (`reg 5 << 9`, 640 bytes) vs RAM `$FF0550` of the snapshot: 0 differing bytes in all map frames of the Ryuma and death replays, 4 frames in Mercator (room 632, Start menu fades, which write sprites directly), 79 bytes in title / file select frames (not map).
- **Result (compare mode, sum of differing pixels, old two-snapshot way -> snapshot):** quick-load 11079 -> 5698; intro recording 398342 -> 392673; Ryuma 24510 -> 20622; death 48424 -> 46967; waterfall 113688 -> 95675; Mercator 104301 -> 100366; frames above 3000 pixels: none (before: 1). Script: loop over the recordings with `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_LOG=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_VB=0|1`, sum field 8 of the `own: frame` lines (the printed frame number is the frame AFTER the compared one).
- **'red flash' (user question):** `DoVisualEffect $00E110` effects: 0 / 1 warp pad, 2 / 3 tree warp, 4 black flash, 5 white flash, 6 red flash, 7..10 Nole warp. The red flash runs 10 times in one cutscene action (`dialogueactions.asm` near `SND_Fireball1` + `LoadMagicSwordEffect`); which scene exactly was not determined.

## Item Info garbage in the Use list (2026-10-21)
 * User: 'im Item Use Menü nach unten scrollen: Grafikmüll statt der Grafiken, im eigenen Renderer'. Test base: `~/aufnahmen/save_20261019_2215.srm` (31 items, late game) + `rec_20261019_2215.txt` frames <= 450 (file select, quick load `LANDSTALKER_QSAVE=1 LANDSTALKER_QSREQ=l400`) + own input: START at 700 (menu fully open ~780), A (`0040`) at 900 = into the list (the Use tab does NOT react to DOWN, the first A enters the list), then DOWN (`0002`, 8 frames held, 20 apart). Pad bits: UP 1 DOWN 2 LEFT 4 RIGHT 8 B $10 C $20 A $40 START $80. Screenshots with `LANDSTALKER_SHOT=...` (headless), contact sheets with `ffmpeg ... -vf tile=3x2` (no PIL here).
 * Bisect: `LANDSTALKER_OWN=0` / `2` give byte-identical screenshots (own renderer innocent, menus are the game's picture), `LANDSTALKER_ITEMINFO=0` is clean, `=1` shows the garbage once the list is scrolled to its end: rows below the cursor are full of `AAAA` / `BBAB` text pixels (the divider line colour `$B`).
 * Evidence: `--ram-dump` at frame 1200, ITEMINFO 0 vs 1: VRAM differs only in the window tile map (`$F420..$FA4B`), work RAM differs in `$FF2020..$FF2604` (window tile map RAM rows 9..20, stride `$80`) and `$FF3900..$FF434B`. `g_Buffer = $FF2C00` (ram.inc), `InventoryMenu_DrawList $00D828` copies `g_Buffer+$84 + scroll * $48` (17 rows x 36 cells) to `g_InventoryTilemap $FF1D80`, so the list rows are read from `$FF2C84 + row * $48` and rows beyond `($FF3900 - $FF2C84) / $48 = 44` live INSIDE the text tile buffer. 31 items = 16 item rows x 4 tile rows = 64 rows.
 * The original game draws the bar text once at menu open (DMA `$0900` words from `$FF3900` to VRAM `$A500`, seen with the QueueDMAOp log) and builds the list afterwards, so it never needs the buffer again; Item Info needs it at every cursor move.
 * Fix (`patch_iteminfo.py` v3): see INDEX status 2026-10-21. Things that did not work: only shrinking the DMA (garbage stays, the rows themselves are overwritten), `FlushDMACopyQueue` before drawing (not the cause). The first version of the release wrapper freed the buffer on every `ProcessQueuedDMAOps` and broke the half drawn bar: `$0234A8` and friends wait for VBlanks inside a slice (flag `lk_ii_inslice`).
 * Not changed / open: with the original game the first bar draw at menu open itself destroys the first `$584` bytes of the buffer's text tiles (harmless, the DMA already ran).

## Own renderer: fade level from the game's counters (2026-10-21)
 - Measuring script (kept in the session scratchpad, easy to rebuild): run the six recordings in parallel (`rec_20261019_2215` with `LANDSTALKER_QSAVE=1 LANDSTALKER_QSREQ=l400`, `rec_20261020_1841` with a missing save file = new game, `rec_20261012_2057` Ryuma, `rec_20261012_2204` death, `rec_20261012_2148` waterfall, `rec_20261016_0039` Mercator with `LANDSTALKER_QUICK=1 LANDSTALKER_QUICK_SLOTS=0,19`; all `LANDSTALKER_WIDE=16 LANDSTALKER_TALL=1`, saves = the `save_<stamp>.srm` next to the recording, copied) with `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_LOG=1 --headless --frames N`, parse `own: frame F room R lines 0..223: D of T` (match on the regexp, other `own:` lines exist) and compare per frame between two builds (the printed frame number is the frame after the compared one). The whole run takes ~75 s in parallel.
 - Facts: `DarkenColour $0009FC` subtracts `d3` (the level) from every colour component (blue `$0E00` units, green d3 >> 4, red d3 >> 8); `DarkenPalette $0009CE` loads d3 from `$FF0F50`, darkens d5 + 1 colours (room fades: `$39` = 58 colours, `DarkenActivePalette`: 64), queues the whole active palette DMA, then `$FF0F50 += $FF0F52`. `FadeInFromDarkness $008EB4` = `InitFadeFromBlackParams` (level `$C00`, step `-$200`) + 7 calls with `Sleep 2`; `FadeOutToDarkness $008EF4` = level `$200`, step `+$200`, 7 calls. White fades (`FadeToWhite`) keep their level in registers and do not touch these counters (the own renderer leaves white flashes to the game).
 - The palette on screen after a call is `$FF0F50 - $FF0F52` (frame 172.. of the quick-load recording: C00, A00, 800 ... 0 each shown for 2 frames). Counters are stale when the game writes the active palette directly (black right after the quick load restore, intro caption colours): there the check against `$FF0ED0` fails and the old search runs.

## Own renderer: camera constants (2026-10-21)
 - How the picture position is made: image coordinates `u = bu - hs (mod 512)`, `v = bv + vs (mod 256) + line`, `hs / vs` = scroll variables `$FF0100 / $FF0500`; `bu / bv` are multiples of 8 (plane tile grid) and constant from a room load until the camera is re-based. They used to be learned by a decaying vote of `upred + hs` rounded to 8 (`upred = 1024 - (cam2 - cam0)`, `vpred = (cam0 + cam2) / 2 - 104`; camera `$FF1200 / $FF1202`).
 - Analysis aid (removed again, re-add if needed): print per room load `(x, y)` = `$FF1124 / $FF1125`, `hsA / vsA`, the vote `bu / bv`. At a load the registers show `hsA = hsB = 144`, `vsA = vsB = -96` (= `InitScrollRegs $00906C`: `hs = (b1 - b0) + $90`, `vs = ((b0 + b1) >> 1) - $68` with the bytes `$FF1126 / $FF1127` = 8, 8). Fitting the settled votes of 44 loads: `ku = bu / 8 = 18 + 2 (x - y) mod 64`; `kv = bv / 8 = (x + y) mod 32` (the first fit gave `x + y + 10` and every kv was 80 px off, i.e. exactly 10 cells: fitted on three points only, then checked on all loads).
 - Statistics of the vote's corrections (`du / dv` between prediction and vote, 6 recordings): room loads with the camera variables still 0 (cam 0 0 while the registers count 0, 2, 4 ...): garbage predictions, harmless because the display is not showing the map yet; steady `dv = -1` for a whole room (room 179: odd `cam0 + cam2`, thousands of frames) and `dv = 4` (one scroll step) while moving: the registers are right, the camera variables lag.
 - Scroll variables vs VDP: equal in 2599 of 2600 frames of the quick load recording, differences only after a quick load restore, after the Gaia effect (VSRAM keeps the shake for 3-4 frames) and inside it (`GaiaEffectLoop` writes VSRAM directly).
 - Not solved: a closed formula for the re-base of looping rooms (room 421: the registers continue while the camera jumps by the loop length); the vote handles it (jump > 48 px resets it).

## Own renderer: visual effects (2026-10-21)
 - Test recordings: tree warp `rec_20261017_1338` (rooms 537 -> 539, frames 1183-1687 with `save_20261017_1338.srm`, `LANDSTALKER_WIDE=16 LANDSTALKER_TALL=1`, 2100 frames), warp pad `rec_20261018_2127` (room 486 -> 148 at frames 451-626; needs `LANDSTALKER_QUICK=1 LANDSTALKER_QUICK_SLOTS=0,19 LANDSTALKER_QSAVE=1 LANDSTALKER_QSREQ=l370` and 900 frames). Compare with `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_LOG=1` (print = frame AFTER the compared one); a coverage listing (runs of frames where the own renderer drew vs the game's picture: `0 of 0 pixels differ` = game) shows at once which frames are still not drawn. Screenshots: `LANDSTALKER_OWN=0` vs `=2` + `ffmpeg ... -vf tile=` for contact sheets; a 20 line PNG diff / best-shift script (zlib only, no PIL) found the 16 px shift of the tree warp (best shift per row = 16, constant) in one go.
 - Tree warp = `DoVisualEffect(2 / 3)` -> `TreeWarpFx`: `sub_E446` fills the whole hscroll table (256 entries x 2 words, `GetSine` of a counter) and queues the DMA, `sub_E4C0` / `sub_E48E` darken the active palette every 4th frame, `MaskVDPReg / OrVDPReg 0x0B` switch the scroll mode. The effect is called twice from `WarpToRoom` (out, then the room loads while black, then in): the first half under `HandleFloorWarpPad $0062C6 (call stack entry $0062EA)`, the second half straight from `GameLoop` (stack `GameLoop $0016DC -> DoVisualEffect $00E110 -> TreeWarpFx`).
 - Warp pad = `WarpPadFx`: copies `g_Pal0Base` (56 colours) to `g_Buffer`, makes 7 more copies each darker by one level (component - 2), then 81 frames of `sub_E1DE`: thresholds `((s + 6 i)^2 >> 4)` (`$DF` when >= `$B7`), `WaitUntilVBlank`, wait for V counter `$FF`, then `sub_E24E` per band i = 0..7 with palette `7 - i`: skipped when the threshold < `$17`, else write the 56 colours to CRAM and wait (HV counter polling) until V + 1 >= threshold; V >= `$B7` ends the frame's bands early. Result per frame: lines [0, t_first) palette of the first valid band, ..., the last valid band's palette stays to the bottom (and into the next frame until its first band is rewritten).
 - Pitfalls: (1) the first snapshot after the effect started can hold a stale `g_Buffer` (the table is computed before the first VBlank wait, but with the very first frame of the effect on the stack it was `BA AA AA BA ...`): the monotone check rejects it; (2) the game's active palette copy is NOT the displayed palette during / right after the warp pad (CRAM was written directly, the copy stays bright): the displayed palette after the effect is black until the fade-in darkens the copy, tracked with a hold flag; (3) the camera vote must not run while `$FF0FC7 & 3 != 0` (first hscroll entry = swirl, not the camera); (4) `lk_o_blanked` (room loaded) never became 1 after a warp without a display blank -> the whole next room was the game's picture.
 - Remaining differences: bottom lines (200..215) of the tree warp swirl-in: the game's plane wraps vertically and shows foliage there; the own image is empty outside the map.

## Own renderer: no VDP any more (2026-10-21)
 - Facts used: the VBlank handler `HandleVBlankInterrupt $0011DE` = `RefreshVDPSpriteTable`, `ProcessQueuedVDPActions $001260` (= `ProcessVRAMCopyQueue $00126C`, `ProcessVRAMReadOp`, `ProcessQueuedDMAOps $001340`), `EnableDisplay`. DMA op = 16 bytes (see symbol `g_DMAOpQueue`); command words: VRAM write `$4000 | addr & $3FFF`, `$0080 | addr >> 14`; VSRAM write `$4000`, `$0090`; CRAM write `$C000`, `$0080`; hscroll op = `$500000083` (VRAM `$D000`, source `$FF0100`, `$200` words). Copy queue entry: word 1 = length - 1 with bit 15 (increment `$40`) / bits 15 + 14 (`$80`), word 2 = VRAM address, data words; the per-frame hscroll update goes through THIS queue (2 words), not through the DMA: a mirror of the DMA ops alone showed `hsA = 144` for ever.
 - Pitfalls: (1) `bus_read8` inside a VBlank wrapper = bus accesses advance the bus clock, the watchdog / VBlank re-enter: the run hung at 100 % CPU (gdb: `bus_read8 <- lk_pq_h <- ProcessQueuedVDPActions`); read RAM through `bus_get_ram()` (index `^ 1`) and the ROM through `bus_get_rom()` (also `^ 1`: byte swapped host copy, without it 7500 frames were 1.3 M pixels worse). (2) Two copies of the same `func_table_register` line in a patch (an old insert left behind) = `lk_pq_o` looked itself up: endless recursion, SIGSEGV after thousands of frames of `lk_pq_h` in the backtrace. (3) The snapshot is taken at the VBlank, registers / state the main code changes AFTER it are only in the live RAM (display flag!), use the live shadow. (4) `pkill -f` / `pgrep -f landstalker` match the own shell command line: use `pgrep -x landstalker`.
 - Measurement: `measure.sh` (6 recordings in parallel, 75 s) + `cmp.py` (per-frame before / after) + `cov.py` (runs of frames drawn by the own renderer vs the game's picture) as in 'Own renderer: visual effects'; `0 of 0 pixels differ` = the game's picture. A frame list of 'worse' frames after a change is the fastest regression finder; pictures of the worst frame side by side + the 20 line PNG shift script show the cause in minutes.
 - Not ported / known differences: Nole / Kazalt warp; the flashes (no recording); the vertical plane wrap at the bottom edge of a map (VDP shows plane rows from the top of the window, the own image is black there: tree warp swirl in, room 111 after the white fade); HUD corner pixels in the first frames of a fade from white (colour 63 / palette line 3); `lk_ot_check` (text mirror vs VRAM) and `LANDSTALKER_OWN_VBLOG` still read the VDP, but only as debug output.

## Own renderer: Fire sword / sword effects (2026-10-22)
 - Recording `rec_20261022_0602` (= `docs/replays/all_swords_room166.txt` + `.chk`; save only on the Deck: `~/aufnahmen/save_20261022_0602.srm`; WIDE 16 + TALL 1, quick items `LANDSTALKER_QUICK=1 LANDSTALKER_QUICK_SLOTS=0,19`): room 166, the user changes through the swords (RAM `$FF114E`: Gaia 180-810, Magic 810-3180, Thunder 3180-4140, Ice 4140-5040, Gaia 5040-6030, Magic 6030-). The replay is deterministic run to run but diverges from the live run at ~frame 2760 (gold in the checkpoint lines); fine for compare mode.
 - Method: `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_LOG=1` (one `own:` line per frame; `$8` = differing pixels), `LANDSTALKER_SHOT=f,f` + a small PNG diff / crop script (no PIL on the Deck: zlib + struct), `LANDSTALKER_OWN_SAT=1` (sprite table entries with covered / EXTRA), `LANDSTALKER_OWN_ENTS=1` (entities: score against the table, pieces of the ones with score 0), `--ram-dump DIR:K` for the struct fields. Pitfall: `LANDSTALKER_OWN=2` (the own picture in the shot) can run a different game state from `=1` after a while (the same frame number showed different scenes at 2849); compare numbers and shots of mode 1 only, or crops of both modes only where the scenes agree (2660, 2772, 2855, 5600 did).
 - Frame numbering: the `own: frame N` line and the shot of frame N-1 are the same picture; `ownents / ownsat: frame N` belong to the `own:` line of N + 1.
 - Finding 1 (FIXED): FIRE SWORD (Sword of Magic). Burning enemies (entities with `+9 = 02`, `+C = 83`) are drawn by the game as effect sprites: sprite table entries `$C7A4 / $C7B4` (palette line 2, priority, sizes F and D, 8 lines below the entity's normal pieces) with tiles loaded dynamically (flames, then the grey stone pose). The own renderer drew the ROM frame of the entity (score 0 against the table; the pieces are 4 x 4 + 4 x 3 cells, the table's are 4 x 4 + 4 x 2) and drew the table's entries as EXTRA after (behind) the figure. Fix: see INDEX status 2026-10-22 (`lk_o_near`). Dead end documented: hiding every entity without any table match made 57 frames worse because the snapshot of the table is sometimes half built (16 of 26 entries; the entities that are not in it yet are real); the 'near' condition (a same-size entry in the same column within 16 lines) separates the two cases. Nigel is excluded (hiding him changed 11 frames by +-3392 px in total).
 - Finding 2 (not fixed): the recurring 5000-9000 px frames (every 3-9 frames in the spike room 166, in ALL sword phases). They always follow a frame in which the game's state did not advance (the animation counters `$FF1134 + 4 k + 2` show the same value twice: frames 2741 / 2742, 2755 / 2756, 2771 / 2772, 2775 / 2776) = a frame with two passes or none. In those frames the game's picture misses Nigel or shows an enemy in the stone pose (the VDP's sprite table / tiles are of a half built pass) while the own picture shows the complete current state; both are plausible on hardware, the own picture is the more consistent one. Also seen: the water tiles (room with the water floor, frame 2030) and the spikes are one animation phase apart in some frames (`ownanim` shows the groups' state; index changes did NOT line up with the big diffs).
 - Not an effect: HUD row 23 (sword gauge bottom edge) differs by 12-50 px in many frames (pre-existing, DEBUGGING 'HUD ...').
 - Thunder / Ice / Gaia (windows listed in INDEX): no systematic error beyond finding 2; no palette flash in this recording (`LANDSTALKER_OWN_PAL=1`: only the fade windows differ from the ROM palette). The flashes (`BlackFlash` / `WhiteFlash` / `RedFlash`, `DoVisualEffect $00E110`) still have no recording that triggers them.
 - Follow-up (2026-10-22, recording `rec_20261022_0706`): the effect sprites (EXTRA table entries) were drawn after all figures = always behind the enemy; the game's order is the table's. Fixed by drawing the figures twice (pass 1 = covered set, pass 2 = in table order with the uncovered entries flushed before each figure, `lk_o_extras_upto`). Numbers in INDEX status. `ownsat` debug lines now print in pass 2 only. No effect entity exists (entity types stay in {93, 68, 5D, 00} during hits): the effect is the enemy's own state (`+9 = 02`, `+C = 83`) plus table entries.


## Own renderer: Nole / Kazalt warp and flashes (2026-10-22)
 - Facts: `DoVisualEffect $00E110` numbers 7 / 8 = Nole warp out / in (`HandleFloorWarpPad $0062C6` -> `loc_62EA` in room 170 `KN_CAVE_TELEPORTER_TO_KAZALT`, and from room 173 back), 9 / 10 = the same effect in the cutscenes `CSA_0103` / `CSA_0106` (effect number - 2 = 7 / 8 inside `NoleWarp $00E56A`), 4 / 5 / 6 = black / white / red flash (they only write the active palette, no loop). `NoleWarp`: the out half runs 600 frames (`d7 = $258`), the in half 360 (`$168`); per frame `UpdateControllerInputs`, `NoleWarp_Particles $00E8F8` (40 sprites written to the sprite table RAM `g_VDPSpr30_Y`: Y word, `$8100` attribute = tile `$100` = VRAM `$2000` (the spark tile built at `$FF2C00` and DMAed once), X word; Y can exceed 511: the VDP uses `Y & $1FF` outside interlace), `NoleWarp_Brighten $00E8A8` every 4th frame (`g_Pal0Base` brightened into `g_Pal0Active`), `NoleWarp_Frame $00E87A` (warp sound, `ContinueKazaltWarpFx` = white flashes after frame `$172`), `QueueFullPaletteDMA`. The out half ends with `clr.w g_Pal0Active` (backdrop black) and the restored palettes; between the halves `sub_A0C2` / `LoadRoom_0` / `InitVDP` run (display blanked), the in half starts by filling the active palette with `0x0EEE (colour)` (white).
 - Test setup (headless, no story needed): `LANDSTALKER_VFX="7,8" LANDSTALKER_VFX_AT=1500` in the Massan replay (`docs/replays/massan.txt`, church save) calls the effects from inside `UpdateSprites` (compare mode `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_LOG=1`, 3200 frames); the real warp: `LANDSTALKER_WARP="170:11.08,11.08,10" LANDSTALKER_WARP_AT=1200 LANDSTALKER_PAD_AT=1300` (the pad cell of room 170 / 173 is (11, 11): `romroom.arrays(...)['HM']` cell with floor type 8; `HandleFloorWarpPad` wants `Player_Z == ground height * 16` and the 'last floor type' byte `$FF1209` != 8, the aid sets both). Walking onto the pad in a replay did not work (no input mapping tried further). Contact sheets with `ffmpeg -pattern_type glob -i 's8/*.png' -vf scale=176:-1,tile=6x6`, pixel diff of two shots with a 20 line zlib PNG reader (rows histogram: the margin / HUD / bottom rows show up at once).
 - Findings: (1) the only missing piece was the map rule (`lk_wfxk = 3` in `lk_map_decide`, `tools/patch_wide.py`): without it the whole effect was the game's picture, with it compare mode shows max 43 differing px (baseline 32) over 1705 frames of effect 7 + 8; the flashes 4 / 5 / 6 max 33 / 43 / 42. (2) 8 frames of 28928 differing px at the end of the out half: the own picture had a black backdrop in a white screen. Cause: `clr.w g_Pal0Active` already ran while CRAM still held the white palette (DMA of the active copy happens at the next VBlank after `QueueFullPaletteDMA`); the direct palette now comes from `lk_cm` (CRAM mirror of the DMA queue, `patch_ownvb.py`), `LANDSTALKER_OWN_CMCHK=1` proves mirror == VDP CRAM (0 differing frames in 2200). (3) one spark per frame missing: Y 756 = 244 on the screen (`& $1FF`), `patch_own.py` masked with `$3FF`. (4) margins next to the HUD bar showed black where the game's picture shows the backdrop colour (white in a flash): `lk_o_pix[LK_REG(7)]` for them (black otherwise: entry 0 is always 0 in the room palettes). (5) remaining difference of the in half: rows 200..223 at the bottom of the map (up to 3885 px at the very end): VDP plane wrap, the own picture shows the true map continuation (same as the known tree warp / room 111 item). Regression: six recordings have identical differing pixel sums before / after (all swords 3850893, sword directions 496356, Mercator 56668, tree warp 275795, warp pad 24043).
 - Not tested on a real Kazalt trip (late game): the user's check is open; the cutscene warps 9 / 10 only through the test aid.

## Own renderer: stage 1, generic screen renderer (2026-10-22)
 - Idea: all picture screens of the game are plain VDP pictures (tile planes, window, sprites), so ONE line composer replaces the per-screen drawers. `lk_scr_line` in `tools/patch_own.py` is a small re-implementation of the line renderer of the VDP for the mode the game uses (H40 = 320 px, 224 lines, no interlace, no shadow / highlight; any other mode returns 0 = the game's picture stays). It is called from `lk_own_row` (hook before the row overlays) for every row the own map path does not draw. Layers: plane B, plane A (or the window plane where the window covers the cell: vertical window `line < 8 * v` / `>= `, horizontal `cell < 2 * h` / `>=`, union), sprites; order from the top: sprite high, A high, B high, sprite low, A low, B low, backdrop; colour index 0 transparent; CRAM word (9 bit `BBBGGGRRR`) -> `pixel_lut[1]`. Sprites: link list from 0, at most 80, the first `viewport.w >> 4` (20; more in the wide picture) that touch the line, at most `viewport.w >> 3` cells (40), a sprite with x field 0 stops the line once a sprite with x != 0 was drawn, Y uses 9 bits, tiles are column major (`tile + column * height + row`), flips as the VDP. hscroll table at `reg13 & $3F << 10` (mode 0 full, 2 per 8 lines, 3 per line; mode 1 = prohibited -> bail out), vscroll from VSRAM (full or per 2 cells = 4 bytes per column pair, A then B).
 - Measuring: `LANDSTALKER_OWN=1 LANDSTALKER_OWN_SCRLOG=1` prints `ownscr: frame F screen: D of T pixels differ in L lines` for every frame the renderer handled (compare only, the game's row is not touched); results: 0 differing pixels in 29 recordings of `~/aufnahmen` (+ `docs/replays`, + credits / lithograph test aids), see INDEX status. Which frames are still the game's picture (counts per recording): reason 4 (display off) was the biggest class (569 of 36000 frames in the new-game replay, 958 of 4800 in the Mercator one) and is covered now, reason 3 (Start menu running with the display on: 21 / 42 frames) and the full-height band lines (`lk_tall_keep`: lines >= 185 of map frames, the band does not come from the VDP state) are not.
 - Pitfalls found: (1) map frames the own map path skips (menu fades) must NOT go through the screen renderer line 185+ in the full-height picture: the game's band there is drawn by `patch_tall.py` while the VDP has the display off (`lk_tall_keep(line)`); the first try showed 11000 differing pixels in the Start menu fade. (2) with WIDE >= 32 the GPGX picture has a glitch at the 320 px column boundary (one column of the plane / window wraps) - the screen renderer is the clean one; compare counts those pixels. (3) sprite budget in the wide picture (`viewport.w >> 4` sprites, `>> 3` cells) did not change the measured differences but is the VDP's rule in this build.

## Own renderer: stage 2, tile origins (2026-10-22)
 - Facts: `DecompressLZ77Gfx $004AB2` (a0 = compressed block in the ROM, a1 = RAM destination, a1 = end of the output on return) is the loader of all compressed tile graphics: `LoadCompressedGfx` (then `DoDMACopy`), `DecompressAndQueueGfxCopy` (then `QueueDMAOp`), `GetTileset`, the sprite loaders. Typical sources in a new game: title / logos `$0386E4`, `$03DF8A`, `$039ED8`, `$03A564`, `$03BCAE` (1.5 .. 12.5 KB each), menu / file select art `$00FB52`, `$00FD5C`, HUD `$009242`, `$142E30`; the room map data `$08FD75` goes through the same function (29952 bytes into `$FF7C02`, not tile art: it only adds meaningless table entries).
 - Pitfall: the recompiled function ends in TAIL JUMPS (`func_table_tail(...); return;`): a wrapper that calls the original through its function pointer only sees the head, `a1` was still the start (0 bytes) - the first version of the table stayed empty. Fix: the wrapper registers the original again, calls it with `func_table_jsr(0x004AB2, ret)` (ret = the return address on the 68K stack), registers itself again and ends with `func_table_rts()` like the other replaced functions.
 - Why by content: tiles are matched by the hash of their 32 bytes (`lk_org_hash`, byte swapped RAM / VRAM arrays hash the same), so the origin is independent of VRAM slot and transfer path; identical tiles of different blocks keep the first origin (counter in the entry). Works for sprite tiles loaded through the same function too. Not covered: tiles the game builds itself (text glyphs composed by `ProcessChar`, the options menu / Item Info tiles of our patches) and ROM data DMAed without decompression.
 - Replacement hook (same day): `lk_rep_get(tile)` asks `lk_org_lookup` for the tile's origin and looks (rom, index), then (rom, 0xFFF = whole block) up in a hash table (`lk_rep_tab`, 8192 slots, filled from `LANDSTALKER_TILETEST` and the tile pack directory at the first screen frame); the result is cached per tile and frame. `lk_scr_ci` sets `lk_scr_rgb` (-1 = game's pixel); plane cells and sprites return `0x1000000 | rgb` instead of a palette index, the compositor writes `0xFF000000 | rgb` for those. Verified by screenshots (OWN=2): magenta title / logos, green SEGA tiles from files; the unreplaced screens stay pixel exact. The pixel diff helper (`pngdiff.py`, zlib only) shows the replaced region exactly.
 - HD picture (same day): lessons: (1) baked RGBA tiles break under palette effects: the first pack was dumped while the SEGA logo was in its raster palette animation / a fade and the file select showed broken frames (missing borders); fix = palette INDEX packs (`.idx`) coloured by the live palette. (2) The HD image has to be built from the FINAL 1x frame (overlays draw after the own renderer's hook): per pixel `final == compositor pixel` decides between the HD block and a repeated pixel. (3) `lk_hd_gen` is bumped at line 0 of every frame in `lk_own_row`; `lk_hd_stamp[line] == lk_hd_gen` at present time = this line was composed this frame. (4) PNG helper of `patch_shot.py` (`lk_shot_png`) writes the HD shots. (5) `rm -rf` of a scratch folder after `cd` is refused by the harness: write shots into fresh folders instead of cleaning old ones.

## HD graphics: map path, options row, filters, HUD specks (2026-10-23, Sonnet 5.5, dev ced859a)
- Yes / No arrow missing (`rec_20261022_2150`, Fahl, room 676): only the own renderer, the compare mode showed 67 px in every blink phase. The arrow is a high priority SAT sprite, the compositor let every opaque window pixel win. Fix: `lk_o_win` returns the window priority bit, a high priority sprite beats a low priority window pixel in the TEXT BOX lines only (the same rule in the HUD lines or for low priority sprites doubled the compare sums: 407849 px over the Ryuma replay). The recording replays exactly only with the user's real `~/.landstalker_*` files (scratch HOME with copies); the default own picture needs `LANDSTALKER_OWN` unset (`=1` shows the game's picture).
- HD map path: plane / window / sprite tiles are looked up by content (`lk_org_lookup_raw`), figure graphics are taught to the origin table (`lk_org_learn`, chunk list address + tile index, FORMATS 'Figure graphics'): sprite tile origins 62 % -> 95 % (Ryuma replay, empty tiles not counted). HD margins: rows 416 wide. Cost headless 3000 frames: 6.6 s (HD Off) -> 13.5 s (HD 4x).
- CRT filters on the HD picture: `lk_hdn` in `tools/display_core.c`; offline check `displaytest --hdfx` (CRT Pro mean diff 4.6 / 255 vs the 1x chain; cost at 1280 x 800 single thread 20 -> 30 ms).
- HUD specks in HD: the quick item overlay rebuilds the HUD bar rows 5..22 after the compositor; per-pixel 'final == compositor' accepted equal colours by chance (507 bad pixels). Fixed with a 3 x 3 neighbourhood test in `lk_hd_assemble`. Debug trick that found it: print pick / colour per line at one x in the map loop, compare with the shot's column.


## Seamless Doors: white veil, walk on, time-free room load (2026-10-23 evening, Opus 5.5, dev b4f96eb .. 1748e2b)
Recordings: `rec_20261023_1937` (church -> 592 -> 424 -> 425 -> 426 -> 177 / 178), `rec_20261023_2012`, `_2044`, `_2120`, `_2131` (592 <-> 424 several times). All with WIDE 16 + TALL, Seamless Doors On, own renderer On, HD On without a pack (inactive), CRT Pro.
- White veil: three neighbour fill rules acted inside the room (transparent doorways, black pixels, seam fill of black outlines). Fix: void = no cell / empty / blank tile CONNECTED to the map's outside (`lk_voidmap` flood fill); check: centre of normal frames byte identical to Seamless Off (was 7000+ px). Doorway at 592 23.08,27.08 (user report) was the intermediate 'all-black tile = void' rule.
- 424 / 425 bands: black edge tiles (colour 15 of line 0) counted as picture in the alignment; now void. Alignments cached per room pair, mirrored (symmetric).
- Walk on: arrival moved by the picture alignment + the distance walked during the gap, only over plain floor (height map 0000 = floor, $4000 wall, type != 0 = trigger: 592 33,48 type 3 stops it). Position-triggered scripts can be skipped (soldier in 424, user: 'egal').
- Gap time in real time (offscreen): 400 ms -> 154 ms (no pacing, fewer glide pictures, the game's fade Sleeps skipped by return address, prewarm) -> ~100 ms (time-free LoadRoomPath via `lk_no_charge`; HBlank handler polls the HV counter = needs time; guard `ncguard`) -> 4..5 game frames (door timer `$FF12DF` set to 2, instant VBlank in waits). RAM after the time-free load vs timed: 19 bytes differ (g_VBlankCounter, g_RNG, g_FrameCount, stack), VRAM / CRAM identical.
- One wrong frame at the switch (the 'jump' / 'flicker'): the first frame of the new room used the old gap shift and an image position that is right only from the second frame; fixed with `lk_sm_panwait` + the shift computed before drawing; neighbours kept during the gap.
- Remaining (measured with LANDSTALKER_PSHOT, offscreen, real time): one interval of ~45 ms per door (gap pictures cost 10..14 ms to compose, first-visit alignment 14 ms); Nigel does not animate in the gap; figures of the old room vanish.
- Test hygiene: copy the save fresh before every run (the game writes it; stale saves left the game on the title screen), fresh scratch HOME, `SDL_JOYSTICK_DISABLE=1` for offscreen runs.

## HD-2D effects: depth blur, bloom, vignette, color grade (2026-10-24, dev)
- Depth per pixel = screen row of the ground point (plane pixels: their row; figures: lowest piece row + Z, one Z unit = one row); HUD / text box / black margins have none (0x7FFF). Focus = Nigel's ground row. Raised walls use their screen row (known limit).
- Measured (offscreen, real time, `~/.landstalker_display` = CRT Pro, WIDE 16): present 6.0 ms without effects; first blur version on the 2 x 2 HD picture 12.5 avg / 19 max (stutter); blur on the 1x picture (half resolution copy, summed-area table) 8.2 avg / 10.7 max; plus bloom + grade + vignette (all High) 10.5 avg / 12.7 max, frame max 17-18 ms, 0 frames over 20 ms. Parts per map frame: blur ~2 ms, bloom ~1.2 ms, grade + vignette ~0.5 ms.
- Test aids: `LANDSTALKER_HD_TIME=1` (averages every 300 frames), `LANDSTALKER_HD_DEPTHDBG=1` (depth as grey), `LANDSTALKER_HD_SHOT=f` (`dofshot_*` at 1x, `hdshot_*` with a pack), env `LANDSTALKER_DOF` / `_BLOOM` / `_VIGNETTE` / `_GRADE`.

## Unknowns triage 2026-10-26 (cloud, no ROM; user: 'schau dir die unknowns an, sind die wirklich noch alle unknown? gibt es Namen die wir ergaenzen koennen?')
- Method: no ROM in the cloud, but the lordmir disassembly SOURCE (`git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/lordmir/landstalker_disasm`, scratch only, never committed) has (1) `code/include/ram.inc` = every RAM name WITH its address (`equ $00FFxxxx`), (2) labels `sub_XXXX` / `loc_XXXX` whose number IS the US ROM address, so an unknown address that has such a label can be read and named without the ROM. Grep the source for `^(sub|loc)_ADDR:`; for RAM compare against `ram.inc`.
- Result: 310 entries -> 64 named (new symbols.json entries, `[2026-10-26 unknowns triage]` in the note), 246 settled, 0 open. Most 'unknowns' were never addresses: `scan-docs` registers every `$XXXX` in the docs, i.e. colour words, tile words, VDP register words, VRAM addresses, sizes, Z80 / SRAM offsets, range bounds, short stack addresses.
- New classes in `tools/unknowns.py` (SETTLED, not counted as open by `list` / `sync` / `check_docs`; kept so `scan-docs` does not re-add them): `not-an-address`, `range`, `stack`, `field` (byte inside a named RAM symbol), `inside-symbol` (call / trap / lea site, return address or inner entry inside a named function), `data` (data decoded as code), `log-artifact`.
- Named code: title fade channels `$039A5C..$039DD8` (were wrongly called sword slash helpers), file select / game start helpers `$00F07C..$01007E`, Start menu internals (`CheckForMenuOpen $007624` was only `sub_007624`; `CheckForMenuOpen_UseList $00774A`, `CloseMenuAndReloadRoom $007766` / `_NoFade $00776E`, `InitInv_LoadScreenTilemap $007816`, `InitInv_LoadCursorGfx $00D996`, `InitInv_LoadMenuFont $00D9FC`, `UseItemList_CursorLeft / Right $00D6C8 / $00D6EC`, `EquipGrid_UpdateCursor $00EDAA`), warps (`WarpToRoomWithDoorSound $00620A`, `WarpToRoom_NoFade $006212` + its stub `j_WarpToRoom_NoFade $00042E`, `HandleFloorWarpPad_NoleCheck $0062EA`), `LoadRoom_SetPlayerZ $002896`, `HandleAttack_EnemyLoop $0160B8`, `UpdatePlayerStatusEffects_HealingBoots $016696`, `EntityRiseBySpeed $0189E0`, `EndCredits_ClearScreenBuffer $09F694`.
- Named RAM (ram.inc names where they are real names, own names for `word_` / `byte_` / `unk_` labels): `g_SaveSlot $FF0010`, `g_Pal0Base..g_Pal3Base $FF0080..$FF00E0`, `g_Flags $FF1000`, `g_AdditionalFlags $FF1020`, `g_TalkSoundEffect $FF1154`, `g_RmNum2 $FF1206`, `g_CameraPixelY $FF1202`, `g_SavedGroundHeight / g_SavedFloorType $FF1208 / $FF1209`, `g_SpritePieceBuffer $FF1400`, `JmpInstr1 / JumpInstr2 $FF1850 / $FF1858`, `Player_SubX / SubY / Z / Defence`, ...
- Names only: the generated C gets the new names through `tools/rename_generated.py` (same numbers, no behaviour change); all patch tools address these functions by number (checked with grep). NOT built here (no ROM).
- Round 2 (same day): the settled entries that patches or docs USE got names too (9, INDEX status). Counted in the source: the 'trap site' addresses `$00FA54` / `$00FA9E` / `$0248F8` / `$00DBBE` are the inline SOUND WORD after `trap #0` (the trap is 2 bytes earlier); `recomp_m68k_exception` is given pc = the word. Everything else settled stays unnamed on purpose: colour / tile / VDP values, range bounds, stack slots, bytes of named variables, return addresses of old broken builds.

## Own sound chips, 2026-10-29 (Sonnet 5.5)

Acceptance = `LANDSTALKER_WAV` capture sample identical: 6 recordings x 6000 frames, intro US + EU, quick save / load requests (`LANDSTALKER_QSREQ`, `LANDSTALKER_QSAVE=1`), fast forward, `LANDSTALKER_SND=gpgx`; run time 24.1 s against 24.4 s.
 * Test pitfalls met: (1) a test that reuses one `.srm` between the A and B run is not an A/B test (the second run loads the first run's save): `rm -f` per run; (2) `HOME` changed for a run makes `~/aufnahmen/...` paths wrong: use absolute paths; (3) a build that failed (EXIT 2) leaves the OLD binary: the tests then compare the old build with itself: always check `EXIT 0` first; (4) in a patch script, C text inside a `%`-formatted '''-string needs `\\\\n` for a newline escape and `%%` for a percent sign.
 * The timing model (frame / line clock, DMA stall, Z80 bus waits, interrupts: `bus.c`, `patch_timing`) is the last GPGX dependency of the sound path and of the whole game; explained to the user, plan not written yet (SOUND_PLAN section 9 'NEXT').

## Time trace and timing bench, 2026-10-29 (Sonnet 5.5; TIMING_PLAN T0)
- **Trace:** `LANDSTALKER_TIMETRACE=/path/file` (env read in a constructor of `bus.c`; `patch_timetrace.py` documents the line format). Hooks: `lk_tt_sr` / `lk_tt_hv` (wrappers of `vdp_68k_ctrl_r` / `vdp_hvc_r` used by `mem68k.c`), `vdp_dma_update` (D), `bus_finish_dma` (W), the two FIFO stalls in `vdp_ctrl.c` (F), `z80_read_byte` / `z80_write_byte` (A), `gen_zbusreq_w` / `gen_zreset_w` (B / R), `z80_request_68k_bus_access` (Z), `gamepad_read` (G), `recomp_m68k_interrupt` (I / q), the frame end of `bus_advance_inner` (E), `bus_advance` (J, only with `LANDSTALKER_TIMETRACE_J=1`). Reading a trace: `grep ' I ' f | head` = interrupts; a frame's block ends with its `E` line (the `H` / `Z` group lines of the frame come right before it).
- **The cycle position can exceed 3420:** the 68K runs ahead of the line loop (`cyc` = `m68k.cycles - mcycles_vdp`); a `Z` or `A` line at `cyc` 1514 .. 3000 is normal.
- **Collapsing:** identical neighbouring `S` / `G` / `A` lines on one video line become one line with `x<count>@<last cyc>`; `H` and `Z` are one group line per frame with an FNV hash of every event (an event moved by one cycle changes the hash). A plain line per event was 11 MB per 4 800 frames (`rec_20261016_0039`) and 52 MB for the 12 000 frame intro; now 6.5 MB for the intro.
- **Bench pitfalls found:** (1) the replay must run `last .chk frame + 1` frames or the last checkpoint line is missing; (2) a missing save file means a NEW game (`LANDSTALKER_SAVE=` pointing nowhere), used for the intro / Massan runs; (3) the environment of the calling shell is cleaned of `LANDSTALKER_*` / `GENRECOMP_*` (a stray `LANDSTALKER_WIDE` would change every recording); (4) HOME is a fresh empty directory per run, work dirs are `~/timebench/work/<name>` and are deleted after hashing; (5) `chk=diff@F` is the first differing `.chk` line (a multiple of 120).
- **Why only 17 of 57 recordings replay exactly:** not investigated (task: record, do not fix). Tried: `LANDSTALKER_QUICK=1 LANDSTALKER_QUICK_SLOTS=0,19` (one recording changed, still not exact), the real `~/.landstalker_*` files in the isolated HOME (no change on 3 of 3 tested). `rec_20261016_0039` (README: 'replays EXACTLY') parts at frame 3 120 with the binary built BEFORE the trace patch too, so it is a change of the game since 2026-10-16, not of the trace.
- **The J analysis (T1 question):** see TIMING_PLAN section 7; the nesting problem is in `bus_advance` (`m68k.cycles += mc` before the loop, `s_expected_cycles` after it).

## Time doors and the door counters, 2026-10-29 (Sonnet 5.5; TIMING_PLAN T1)
- **What:** `tools/patch_timedoors.py` (marker `LK_TIMEDOORS v1`) routes every GPGX read / move of the 68K clock on Landstalker's paths through `lk_t_*` functions in `bus.c` (list: TIMING_PLAN section 8). `lk_t_door[id]` holds counters and the clock before / after the last call; a later shadow compare reads these.
- **Counters:** `LANDSTALKER_TIMEDOORS=1` (stderr at exit) or `=path` (appended; sum several runs with awk/python, e.g. `timebench.py --env LANDSTALKER_TIMEDOORS=/tmp/td.txt`, the bench discards stdout/stderr so use the path form there). `moved_calls` = calls that moved `m68k.cycles`, `moved_mclk` = sum (master clocks, 7 per 68K cycle), `z80_mclk` = Z80 clock moved.
- **Nesting:** `zbus_w` contains `zwait` moves (the busreq write runs the Z80); `dma_start` shows GPGX's first DMA stall, before `bus_finish_dma` rewrites it.
- **CYCSTAT check without the bench:** call `timebench.jobs_list()` / `clean_env()` from a small script, set `LANDSTALKER_CYCSTAT=1`, keep the output file (the bench deletes its work dirs); per frame RAM/ROM should be ~0 (6 cycles residual after interrupt entries).
- **Pitfall:** `git checkout src/bus.c` inside `.deps/genrecomp` wipes every patch of that file; only a full `build.sh` re-applies them.


## Own clock state and the shadow, 2026-10-29 (Sonnet 5.5; TIMING_PLAN T2)
`lk_clk` in `bus.c` (fields and who pushes / pulls: TIMING_PLAN section 9). Debug: `LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=/tmp/s.txt` then `cat /tmp/s.txt` (mismatch lines with call stack, last line = summary; the bench with `--env LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=path` appends one summary per run, sum them with a small python). Rollback to the old master: `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx`. A mismatch means GPGX changed a clock variable outside a door / the loop (add a pull) or the loop changed one without a push before GPGX read it. Proof that a quick load ran: the `TIME_SHADOW RESYNC (quick load)` line (only one load per request that the game accepts; refused requests leave no line). Quick loop: edit `.deps/genrecomp/src/bus.c`, `cmake --build build -j8` (4 s); to restore after an experiment copy the file back from a saved copy, NEVER `git checkout` in `.deps`.

## Timing T3: VDP port timing ours (2026-10-29 night)
Full design: TIMING_PLAN section 11 (`tools/patch_vdptime.py`, marker `LK_VDPTIME v1`, `lk_v_*` in `bus.c`). Debug: `LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=/tmp/s.txt` computes ours AND GPGX's result at every status read / HV read / DMA chunk / FIFO write / mode check, applies GPGX's, prints the first 6 differences per kind (frame, line, call stack); last line `TIME_SHADOW VDP own=1 status=c/m hvc=c/m dma=c/m fifo=c/m mode=c/m m4_writes=N slot_overrun=N` (c = checks, m = mismatches; sum the per-run lines of a bench with a small python). Rollback: `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx`.
Shadow sanity experiment (temporary edits, restored, binary compared): DMA chunk +7 mclk -> 486 dma mismatches; H phase +9 -> 51 828 hvc mismatches; FIFO slot +1 -> 3 fifo mismatches; status bit 0x200 flipped -> 20 / 20. A V counter `>=` edit and a FIFO rounding edit were NOT detected on `rec_20261016_0039` (it never reads V on line 234 and never fills the FIFO), only by the full bench.
Numbers (62 runs, `~/timebench/baseline.txt`): bench `--compare` IDENTICAL in own (574 s wall), shadow (557 s) and `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx` (560 s); shadow: status 524 702, hvc 4 151 406, dma 1 256 662, fifo 5 211 017, mode 6 992 381 checks, 0 mismatches, m4 writes 0, slot overrun 0, T2 struct checks 162 247 145 with 0 mismatches. Quick save / load + fast forward (`LANDSTALKER_QSAVE=1 LANDSTALKER_QSREQ=s2000,l2400,s3300,l3600 LANDSTALKER_FFWD=1 LANDSTALKER_FFWD_HOLD=1`, `rec_20261016_0039`): RAM / WAV / trace hashes 90ec784e / e50c4cea / a50a37aa equal to the pre-T3 binary (`~/timebench/landstalker_pre_T3`), load at frame 3601 ran (resync, 0 fields differed). Run time A/B 3 runs each: 15.4 / 15.7 / 15.0 s vs 15.8 / 15.6 / 16.0 s = +3 %. Full `build.sh` from scratch done: Build exit 0 (1 min 28 s with the cached .deps), `verify_build.sh` 64 ok, bench with that binary IDENTICAL (62 runs, 556 s wall).
Pitfalls: (1) the bench work dir used to be shared (`~/timebench/work/<recording>`): two benches at once gave a false DIFFERENCE; now per process `~/timebench/work/<pid>/`. (2) a bench with the long recording `rec_20261029_1729` (43 201 frames, 185 s alone) is longer than the 450 s of T2. (3) GPGX's timing code is skipped in own mode: debugging it needs `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx`.


## Timing T4: Z80 bus timing ours (2026-10-29 night)
Full design: TIMING_PLAN section 12 (`tools/patch_z80time.py`, marker `LK_Z80TIME v1`, `lk_z_*` in `bus.c`). Debug: `LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=/tmp/s.txt` computes the Z80 bus wait (`zwait`) ours AND GPGX's at every banked Z80 access; the exit line is `TIME_SHADOW Z80 own=1 zwait=checks/mismatches`. The BUSREQ / RESET handshake cannot run twice (it runs the Z80), so it is checked only by the bench (RAM, WAV, `B` / `R` / `Z` trace lines). `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx` = rollback to GPGX's functions.
Shadow sanity experiment (temporary edit, restored): `72` -> `73` in the 68K wait gave the first mismatch at frame 14 (`Reset_InitHardware`, 68K ours +7).
Numbers (62 runs, `~/timebench/baseline.txt`, binaries `~/timebench/landstalker_pre_T4` / `landstalker_T4`): bench `--compare` IDENTICAL in own (542 s wall), shadow (544 s) and gpgx (547 s); shadow zwait 33 411 857 checks, 0 mismatches (T3 VDP and T2 struct checks again 0). Quick save / load + fast forward on `rec_20261016_0039`: RAM / WAV / trace hashes equal to pre-T4 (90ec784e / e50c4cea / a50a37aa). Run time 3 runs each: pre-T4 15.5 / 16.0 / 15.6 s, T4 15.2 / 15.1 / 15.3 s. Full `build.sh` from scratch: Build exit 0 (1 min 27 s), `verify_build.sh` 65 ok, bench IDENTICAL (542 s).
Pitfalls: (1) if `genesis.c` changes, the fingerprint guard stops the build (see LESSONS section 9). (2) The Z80 boot stub is still GPGX's interpreter (T6).

## Timing T5: pad timing ours (2026-10-29 night)
Full design: TIMING_PLAN section 13 (`tools/patch_padtime.py`, marker `LK_PADTIME v1`, `lk_p_lat[8]`, `lk_t_pad_clr`, `lk_t_input_end` in `bus.c`). Debug: `LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=/tmp/s.txt` computes the TH latency deadline ours AND GPGX's; the exit line is `TIME_SHADOW PAD own=1 pad=checks/mismatches`. `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx` = GPGX's deadline + `input_end_frame`. Landstalker never switches TH to input (`lk_t_pad_w` 0 calls), so the latency branch is never taken; the step is about ownership.
Shadow sanity experiment (temporary edit, restored, binary byte identical again): a deadline of 1 instead of 0 after a pad reset gave the first mismatch at frame 30 (port 0).
Numbers (62 runs, `~/timebench/baseline.txt`, binaries `~/timebench/landstalker_pre_T5` / `landstalker_T5`): bench `--compare` IDENTICAL in own (558 s wall), shadow (559 s) and gpgx (560 s); shadow pad 1 022 884 checks, 0 mismatches (zwait 33.4 M, VDP and T2 struct again 0). Quick save / load + fast forward on `rec_20261016_0039`: RAM / WAV / trace hashes equal to pre-T5 (90ec784e / e50c4cea / a50a37aa). Run time 3 runs each: pre-T5 15.2 / 15.3 / 15.6 s, T5 15.4 / 15.6 / 15.5 s. Full `build.sh` from scratch: Build exit 0 (1 min 28 s), `verify_build.sh` 66 ok, bench IDENTICAL (552 s).
Pitfalls: (1) anchors by exact text in `gamepad_reset` / `gamepad_write` (see LESSONS section 9). (2) In own mode `input_end_frame` is not called.

## Timing T6: power-on time state ours (2026-10-30 night)
Full design: TIMING_PLAN section 14 (`tools/patch_starttime.py`, marker `LK_STARTTIME v1`, `lk_time_power_on()` in `bus.c`). Debug: `LANDSTALKER_TIME_SHADOW=/tmp/s.txt` adds `TIME_SHADOW START own=1 fields=19 mismatches=M`; a difference to GPGX's reset also prints `LK_STARTTIME: <field> differs`. `LANDSTALKER_TIMING=gpgx` = GPGX's reset values stay.
Finding 1: the first hook after `lk_sram_load()` + `recomp_m68k_reset()` gave 5 differing fields (68K 545 062, line 159 ...) and 3 bench differences, because the first bus access of `lk_sram_load` already ran the line loop through a whole frame wrap; moved before `lk_sram_load`. Finding 2: 1 field left, the 68K clock 280 mclk short = the 40 cycle reset exception of Musashi's `m68k_pulse_reset`; with it 19 / 19 equal (NTSC power-on 68K clock 784 462, Z80 784 170).
Quick saves need no timing block (game's own save / load, no `state_save` / `state_load` in `src/`).
Numbers (62 runs, binaries `~/timebench/landstalker_pre_T6` / `landstalker_T6`): bench `--compare` IDENTICAL in own (555 s wall), shadow (564 s), gpgx (567 s) and the full build (565 s, `verify_build.sh` 67 ok); START 19 / 19 equal and 0 mismatches in 62 / 62 runs. Quick save / load + fast forward hashes equal to pre-T6 (90ec784e / e50c4cea / a50a37aa). Run time pre-T6 15.5 / 15.5 / 16.9 / 15.3 s, T6 15.4 / 15.8 / 15.7 / 15.6 / 15.7 s; one pre-T6 run void (the Deck suspended, 2 796 s): keep the Deck on power with auto-suspend off for long runs.
